It's been on my mind a lot, even though my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: How much distress has this concern about your skin been causing you?\n\nPatient: I mean, it's not ruining my life or anything, but it definitely bothers me. It's just... frustrating, I guess. I feel a lot more self-conscious about it now, especially when I'm getting ready for work in the morning. It just kind of nags at me, you know? Like a distraction I can't quite shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about the skin around your cheeks and jaw, or have you started to notice other flaws in your appearance as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just that one area. Um, I haven't noticed anything else that bothers me like that. It's strictly just the skin around my lower face and jaw. Everything else seems fine to me, it's just... that one specific thing that keeps catching my eye.\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend each day thinking about the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I guess I haven't really timed it or anything. Um, I'm not sure exactly. It's mostly just when I'm in the bathroom, like when I'm getting ready in the morning or washing my face at night. Maybe a little bit during the day if I catch my reflection, but I don't think it adds up to hours and hours or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw affected your ability to work or interact with others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still get all my work done. It hasn't stopped me from doing my job. But... I guess the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning has made me rush into work a few times. And I've been late to my yoga class more than once because of it. Socially, I guess it's affected me a little bit. I've skipped out on being in a few casual photos at my book club and some community events. I just... I didn't want to worry about how my face was going to look in the pictures. So, it hasn't stopped my life completely, but it does get in the way sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would notice the unevenness or sagging of the skin around your cheeks and jaw if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not entirely sure. I mean, this all really started after we put in a brighter mirror in our bathroom. That's when I kept noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. But... my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, even when I point it out to him. So I don't know if other people would actually notice it if they looked closely, or if it's just me seeing it in that lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw would lessen if you were to avoid mirrors or reflective surfaces for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't tried doing that. I suppose if I wasn't looking in that bright bathroom mirror every morning, I might not think about it as much. But, um... I don't know if the worry would actually go away, or if I'd just be wondering how it looks instead. Plus, it's kind of hard to avoid mirrors entirely when I have to get ready for the office every day. So, I guess I just don't know for sure if it would help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about trying to fix or change the appearance of the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about fixing it, no. I mean, I guess I've bought some different face creams from the drugstore to see if they help with the unevenness, but I haven't considered anything major like a procedure or surgery. I just try to cover it up with makeup the best I can when I get ready for work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because... I guess it started when we put a new, brighter mirror in our bathroom recently. Ever since we did that, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and my jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. It's been on my mind a lot, even though my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: How much distress has this concern about your skin been causing you?\n\nPatient: I mean, it's not ruining my life or anything, but it definitely bothers me. It's just... frustrating, I guess. I feel a lot more self-conscious about it now, especially when I'm getting ready for work in the morning. It just kind of nags at me, you know? Like a distraction I can't quite shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about the skin around your cheeks and jaw, or have you started to notice other flaws in your appearance as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just that one area. Um, I haven't noticed anything else that bothers me like that. It's strictly just the skin around my lower face and jaw. Everything else seems fine to me, it's just... that one specific thing that keeps catching my eye.\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend each day thinking about the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I guess I haven't really timed it or anything. Um, I'm not sure exactly. It's mostly just when I'm in the bathroom, like when I'm getting ready in the morning or washing my face at night. Maybe a little bit during the day if I catch my reflection, but I don't think it adds up to hours and hours or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw affected your ability to work or interact with others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still get all my work done. It hasn't stopped me from doing my job. But... I guess the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning has made me rush into work a few times. And I've been late to my yoga class more than once because of it. Socially, I guess it's affected me a little bit. I've skipped out on being in a few casual photos at my book club and some community events. I just... I didn't want to worry about how my face was going to look in the pictures. So, it hasn't stopped my life completely, but it does get in the way sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would notice the unevenness or sagging of the skin around your cheeks and jaw if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not entirely sure. I mean, this all really started after we put in a brighter mirror in our bathroom. That's when I kept noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. But... my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, even when I point it out to him. So I don't know if other people would actually notice it if they looked closely, or if it's just me seeing it in that lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw would lessen if you were to avoid mirrors or reflective surfaces for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't tried doing that. I suppose if I wasn't looking in that bright bathroom mirror every morning, I might not think about it as much. But, um... I don't know if the worry would actually go away, or if I'd just be wondering how it looks instead. Plus, it's kind of hard to avoid mirrors entirely when I have to get ready for the office every day. So, I guess I just don't know for sure if it would help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about trying to fix or change the appearance of the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about fixing it, no. I just try to cover it up with makeup the best I can when I get ready for work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to stop thinking about the skin around your cheeks and jaw once you've started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do find it hard to stop thinking about it. Like I mentioned, it really started after we put in that brighter bathroom mirror. I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven or kind of saggy to me. Even though my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, once I see it in the morning, it just kind of stays on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever get unwanted thoughts or images about the skin around your cheeks and jaw popping into your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. It's really just a lingering thought after I actually look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you think about the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I don't think I have complete control over it, no. So, I guess I just don't know for sure if it would help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about trying to fix or change the appearance of the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about fixing it, no. I mean, I guess I've bought some different face creams from the drugstore to see if they help with the unevenness, but I haven't considered anything major like a procedure or surgery. I just try to cover it up with makeup the best I can when I get ready for work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to stop thinking about the skin around your cheeks and jaw once you've started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do find it hard to stop thinking about it. Like I mentioned, it really started after we put in that brighter bathroom mirror. I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven or kind of saggy to me. Even though my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, once I see it in the morning, it just kind of stays on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever get unwanted thoughts or images about the skin around your cheeks and jaw popping into your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really get sudden pictures or thoughts about it just... popping into my head out of nowhere. Like I said, it's more that once I see it in the mirror in the morning, the worry kind of sticks with me. But I wouldn't say I have random images of my cheeks or jaw interrupting me while I'm working at the law firm or anything like that. It's really just a lingering thought after I actually look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you think about the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I don't think I have complete control over it, no. Like I was saying, once I see it in the mirror, it just sort of lingers. I can still do my job and everything, but it's hard to just turn the thought off completely. I wouldn't say it takes over my whole day, but I can't really stop myself from thinking about it once it's there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the skin around your cheeks and jaw are reasonable, or do they seem excessive or irrational to you?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know my husband says he doesn't see it, so maybe it's a little excessive. But to me, it feels real. I don't think it's completely irrational because I can clearly see the unevenness when I look in the mirror. I know I probably focus on it more than most people would, but I wouldn't say it's totally crazy or anything. It just bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness or sagging of the skin around your cheeks and jaw might not be as noticeable to others as it is to you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I suppose it's possible. Like I said, my husband says he doesn't see it, so... maybe other people don't notice it as much as I do. But when I look in the mirror, it's just so obvious to me. I guess I can't say for sure what other people see, but it definitely feels very noticeable to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um, I'm not sure exactly. It's mostly just when I'm in the bathroom, like when I'm getting ready in the morning or washing my face at night. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF45.2, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because... I guess it started when we put a new, brighter mirror in our bathroom recently. Ever since we did that, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and my jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. It's been on my mind a lot, even though my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about.\n\nDoctor: How much distress has this concern about your skin been causing you?\n\nPatient: I mean, it's not ruining my life or anything, but it definitely bothers me. It's just... frustrating, I guess. I feel a lot more self-conscious about it now, especially when I'm getting ready for work in the morning. It just kind of nags at me, you know? Like a distraction I can't quite shake.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern only about the skin around your cheeks and jaw, or have you started to notice other flaws in your appearance as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just that one area. Um, I haven't noticed anything else that bothers me like that. It's strictly just the skin around my lower face and jaw. Everything else seems fine to me, it's just... that one specific thing that keeps catching my eye.\n\nDoctor: How much time would you say you spend each day thinking about the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I guess I haven't really timed it or anything. Um, I'm not sure exactly. It's mostly just when I'm in the bathroom, like when I'm getting ready in the morning or washing my face at night. Maybe a little bit during the day if I catch my reflection, but I don't think it adds up to hours and hours or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw affected your ability to work or interact with others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still get all my work done. It hasn't stopped me from doing my job. But... I guess the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning has made me rush into work a few times. And I've been late to my yoga class more than once because of it. Socially, I guess it's affected me a little bit. I've skipped out on being in a few casual photos at my book club and some community events. I just... I didn't want to worry about how my face was going to look in the pictures. So, it hasn't stopped my life completely, but it does get in the way sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you think that others would notice the unevenness or sagging of the skin around your cheeks and jaw if they looked closely?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm not entirely sure. I mean, this all really started after we put in a brighter mirror in our bathroom. That's when I kept noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. But... my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, even when I point it out to him. So I don't know if other people would actually notice it if they looked closely, or if it's just me seeing it in that lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw would lessen if you were to avoid mirrors or reflective surfaces for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't tried doing that. I suppose if I wasn't looking in that bright bathroom mirror every morning, I might not think about it as much. But, um... I don't know if the worry would actually go away, or if I'd just be wondering how it looks instead. Plus, it's kind of hard to avoid mirrors entirely when I have to get ready for the office every day. So, I guess I just don't know for sure if it would help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about trying to fix or change the appearance of the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about fixing it, no. I mean, I guess I've bought some different face creams from the drugstore to see if they help with the unevenness, but I haven't considered anything major like a procedure or surgery. I just try to cover it up with makeup the best I can when I get ready for work.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find it difficult to stop thinking about the skin around your cheeks and jaw once you've started?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do find it hard to stop thinking about it. Like I mentioned, it really started after we put in that brighter bathroom mirror. I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven or kind of saggy to me. Even though my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, once I see it in the morning, it just kind of stays on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever get unwanted thoughts or images about the skin around your cheeks and jaw popping into your mind?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't really get sudden pictures or thoughts about it just... popping into my head out of nowhere. Like I said, it's more that once I see it in the mirror in the morning, the worry kind of sticks with me. But I wouldn't say I have random images of my cheeks or jaw interrupting me while I'm working at the law firm or anything like that. It's really just a lingering thought after I actually look at it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you think about the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I don't think I have complete control over it, no. Like I was saying, once I see it in the mirror, it just sort of lingers. I can still do my job and everything, but it's hard to just turn the thought off completely. I wouldn't say it takes over my whole day, but I can't really stop myself from thinking about it once it's there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about the skin around your cheeks and jaw are reasonable, or do they seem excessive or irrational to you?\n\nPatient: I guess... I mean, I know my husband says he doesn't see it, so maybe it's a little excessive. But to me, it feels real. I don't think it's completely irrational because I can clearly see the unevenness when I look in the mirror. I know I probably focus on it more than most people would, but I wouldn't say it's totally crazy or anything. It just bothers me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness or sagging of the skin around your cheeks and jaw might not be as noticeable to others as it is to you?\n\nPatient: I mean, I suppose it's possible. Like I said, my husband says he doesn't see it, so... maybe other people don't notice it as much as I do. But when I look in the mirror, it's just so obvious to me. I guess I can't say for sure what other people see, but it definitely feels very noticeable to me.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concerns about the skin around your cheeks and jaw are causing you significant distress or are significantly interfering with your daily life?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say it completely takes over, because I still get my work done. But... the extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings has made me have to rush into the courthouse a few times. And I've actually missed the start of my yoga class more than once because I was stuck in front of the mirror. \n\nI guess it affects me socially a little bit, too. Like, I've skipped being in a few casual photos at my book club and at some community events recently. I just didn't want to have to worry about how my face was going to look in the pictures. So, it does get in the way a little, even if I'm still functioning normally for the most part.\n\nDoctor: Is your concern about your appearance still focused only on the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: Yes, it's really just the skin around my cheeks and jaw. Like I mentioned, it all started when we put in that brighter bathroom mirror. That's when I first started noticing that the skin there looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. Even though my husband says he doesn't see it, that's the only part of my appearance that I'm focused on.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Like I was saying earlier, it doesn't completely stop me from functioning—I still get all my work done. But it definitely causes me some stress. 

The extra time I spend getting ready has made me have to rush into the courthouse a few mornings, and I've missed the start of my yoga class more than once because of it. It's just... frustrating, I guess. I feel a lot more self-conscious about it now, especially when I'm getting ready for work in the morning. It just kind of nags at me, you know? Um, I'm not sure exactly. It's mostly just when I'm in the bathroom, like when I'm getting ready in the morning or washing my face at night. Maybe a little bit during the day if I catch my reflection, but I don't think it adds up to hours and hours or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Has your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw affected your ability to work or interact with others?\n\nPatient: I mean, I still get all my work done. It hasn't stopped me from doing my job. But... I guess the extra time I spend getting ready in the morning has made me rush into work a few times. I mean, this all really started after we put in a brighter mirror in our bathroom. That's when I kept noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. But... my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, even when I point it out to him. So I don't know if other people would actually notice it if they looked closely, or if it's just me seeing it in that lighting.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about the skin around your cheeks and jaw would lessen if you were to avoid mirrors or reflective surfaces for a while?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't tried doing that. I suppose if I wasn't looking in that bright bathroom mirror every morning, I might not think about it as much. But, um... I don't know if the worry would actually go away, or if I'd just be wondering how it looks instead. Plus, it's kind of hard to avoid mirrors entirely when I have to get ready for the office every day. So, I guess I just don't know for sure if it would help.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think about trying to fix or change the appearance of the skin around your cheeks and jaw?\n\nPatient: I haven't really thought about fixing it, no. I haven't tried doing that. I suppose if I wasn't looking in that bright bathroom mirror every morning, I might not think about it as much. But, um... I don't know if the worry would actually go away, or if I'd just be wondering how it looks instead. Well, I think it is, but... I guess I don't know for sure. It really just bothers me when I'm actively looking at it or, you know, when a camera comes out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of the unevenness or sagging of the skin around your cheeks and jaw is an accurate reflection of how it actually looks?\n\nPatient: Well, I think it is, but... I guess I don't know for sure. I mean, ever since we put in a brighter bathroom mirror at the house, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven and kind of saggy to me. But my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about when I point it out to him. So... I definitely see it, but maybe it doesn't actually look as bad to everyone else as it does to me."
